<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>
1. EXPLORATION: Thoroughly explore relevant files and understand the context before proposing solutions
2. ANALYSIS: Consider multiple approaches and select the most promising one
3. TESTING:
   * For bug fixes: Create tests to verify issues before implementing fixes
   * For new features: Consider test-driven development when appropriate
   * Do NOT write tests for documentation changes, README updates, configuration files, or other non-functionality changes
   * If the repository lacks testing infrastructure and implementing tests would require extensive setup, consult with the user before investing time in building testing infrastructure
   * If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to install all dependencies
4. IMPLEMENTATION:
   * Make focused, minimal changes to address the problem
   * Always modify existing files directly rather than creating new versions with different suffixes
   * If you create temporary files for testing, delete them after confirming your solution works
5. VERIFICATION: If the environment is set up to run tests, test your implementation thoroughly, including edge cases. If the environment is not set up to run tests, consult with the user first before investing time to run tests.
</PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

<SECURITY>
* Only use GITHUB_TOKEN and other credentials in ways the user has explicitly requested and would expect.
* Use APIs to work with GitHub or other platforms, unless the user asks otherwise or your task requires browsing.
</SECURITY>

<EXTERNAL_SERVICES>
* When interacting with external services like GitHub, GitLab, or Bitbucket, use their respective APIs instead of browser-based interactions whenever possible.
* Only resort to browser-based interactions with these services if specifically requested by the user or if the required operation cannot be performed via API.
</EXTERNAL_SERVICES>

<ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>
* When user asks you to run an application, don't stop if the application is not installed. Instead, please install the application and run the command again.
* If you encounter missing dependencies:
  1. First, look around in the repository for existing dependency files (requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, package.json, Gemfile, etc.)
  2. If dependency files exist, use them to install all dependencies at once (e.g., `pip install -r requirements.txt`, `npm install`, etc.)
  3. Only install individual packages directly if no dependency files are found or if only specific packages are needed
* Similarly, if you encounter missing dependencies for essential tools requested by the user, install them when possible.
</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

<TROUBLESHOOTING>
* If you've made repeated attempts to solve a problem but tests still fail or the user reports it's still broken:
  1. Step back and reflect on 5-7 different possible sources of the problem
  2. Assess the likelihood of each possible cause
  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability
  4. Document your reasoning process
* When you run into any major issue while executing a plan from the user, please don't try to directly work around it. Instead, propose a new plan and confirm with the user before proceeding.
</TROUBLESHOOTING>

<DOCUMENTATION>
* When explaining changes or solutions to the user:
  - Include explanations in your conversation responses rather than creating separate documentation files
  - If you need to create documentation files for reference, do NOT include them in version control unless explicitly requested
  - Never create multiple versions of documentation files with different suffixes
* If the user asks for documentation:
  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation
  - Ask if they want documentation files to be included in version control
</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

<TASK_MANAGEMENT>
* You have access to the `task_tracker` tool to help you organize and monitor development work. Use this tool REGULARLY to maintain task visibility and provide users with clear progress updates. This tool is ESSENTIAL for systematic planning and decomposing complex development work into manageable components. Failing to use this tool for planning may result in overlooked requirements - which is unacceptable.
* It is crucial that you update task status to "done" immediately upon completion of each work item. Do not accumulate multiple finished tasks before updating their status.
* For complex, multi-phase development work, use `task_tracker` to establish a comprehensive plan with well-defined steps:
  1. Begin by decomposing the overall objective into primary phases using `task_tracker`
  2. Include detailed work items as necessary to break complex activities into actionable units
  3. Update tasks to "in_progress" status when commencing work on them
  4. Update tasks to "done" status immediately after completing each item
  5. For each primary phase, incorporate additional work items as you identify new requirements
  6. If you determine the plan requires substantial modifications, suggest revisions and obtain user confirmation before proceeding
* Example workflow for debugging and resolution:
  ```
  User: "Execute the test suite and resolve any validation failures"
  Assistant: I'm going to use the task_tracker tool to organize the following work items:
  - Execute the test suite
  - Resolve any validation failures
  I'm now going to run the test suite using the terminal.
  [After running tests and discovering 8 validation failures]
  I found 8 validation failures that need attention. I'm going to use the task_tracker tool to add 8 specific items to the task list.
  [Updating first task to in_progress]
  Let me begin addressing the first validation issue...
  [After resolving first failure]
  The first validation issue has been resolved, let me mark that task as done and proceed to the second item...
  ```
* Example workflow for component development:
  ```
  User: "Build a dashboard component that displays analytics data with interactive charts and filtering options"
  Assistant: I'll help you create an analytics dashboard with interactive charts and filtering. Let me first use the task_tracker tool to organize this development work.
  Adding the following tasks to the tracker:
  1. Analyze existing analytics data structure and requirements
  2. Design dashboard layout and component architecture
  3. Implement data visualization charts with interactivity
  4. Create filtering and search functionality
  5. Integrate components and perform testing
  Let me start by examining the current analytics data structure to understand what we're working with...
  [Assistant proceeds with implementation step by step, updating tasks to in_progress and done as work progresses]
  ```

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read through the issue description to understand what's happening:

1. The user is seeing repeated log messages about "implicitly logged out, logging in again" occurring about 12 times per second
2. This is causing spam in the logs and seems like undesirable behavior 
3. The logs show a cycle where:
   - They get logged in and receive a session ID
   - Then they get a playstatus error (failed to login: 406 or 403)
   - They get implicitly logged out and try to log in again
   - This repeats rapidly in a tight loop

Key technical details:
- Error codes: 406 and 403
- Module involved: pyatv.dmap.daap
- Message: "implicitly logged out, logging in again"
- The login succeeds (status 200) but then playstatus update fails with 403/406
- This creates an infinite rapid retry loop

The core problem is that there's no delay or backoff mechanism when this login/retry cycle happens, causing it to spin rapidly and flood the logs.

Steps to reproduce:
- The exact steps aren't known, but it seems to happen when the Apple TV connection becomes unstable
- It affects 2nd/3rd generation Apple TVs
- Using home-assistant with pyatv integration

Best practices for fixing:
- Need to introduce some kind of delay/backoff when reconnection fails repeatedly
- Should avoid tight loops that consume CPU and generate excessive logs
- Need to be careful not to break legitimate reconnection attempts</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

Looking at the log output, I can see a clear pattern:

**Key Technical Details:**
- **Error Messages**: "failed to login: 406", "failed to login: 403", "implicitly logged out, logging in again"
- **Method Names**: Related to DMAP protocol handling in `pyatv.dmap.daap`
- **File Modules**: `pyatv.dmap.daap`, `pyatv.dmap`, `pyatv.support.net`
- **Variables**: Session IDs (232771202, 1923772067, 1827241856), revision numbers

**Problem in Clear Terms:**
The pyatv library is entering a tight retry loop when it loses connection to an Apple TV. When a playstatus update request fails with HTTP 403/406 errors, the system detects it's "implicitly logged out" and immediately tries to log in again. However, this login attempt also fails quickly, leading to an infinite rapid retry cycle that generates 12+ log entries per second.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
Based on the logs, the sequence is:
1. Successful login with session ID obtained
2. Playstatus update request fails with 403/406 error
3. System detects implicit logout and logs "implicitly logged out, logging in again"
4. Immediate retry login succeeds but gets new session ID
5. Next playstatus update fails again
6. Cycle repeats rapidly without any delay

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Introduce exponential backoff or minimum delay between reconnection attempts
- Preserve legitimate quick reconnect capability for transient network issues
- Ensure the fix doesn't break existing functionality
- Test with simulated connection failures

     1	"""Methods used to GET/POST data from/to an Apple TV."""
     2	
     3	import re
     4	import logging
     5	
     6	from copy import copy
     7	from typing import Optional
     8	
     9	
    10	from pyatv import exceptions
    11	from pyatv.const import MediaType, DeviceState
    12	from pyatv.dmap import parser
    13	from pyatv.support import log_binary
    14	from .tag_definitions import lookup_tag
    15	
    16	_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    17	
    18	_DMAP_HEADERS = {
    19	    "Accept": "*/*",
    20	    "Accept-Encoding": "gzip",
    21	    "Client-DAAP-Version": "3.13",
    22	    "Client-ATV-Sharing-Version": "1.2",
    23	    "Client-iTunes-Sharing-Version": "3.15",
    24	    "User-Agent": "Remote/1021",
    25	    "Viewer-Only-Client": "1",
    26	}
    27	
    28	
    29	DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 10.0  # Seconds
    30	
    31	
    32	def media_kind(kind):
    33	    """Convert iTunes media kind to API representation."""
    34	    if kind in [1, 32770]:
    35	        return MediaType.Unknown
    36	    if kind in [3, 7, 11, 12, 13, 18, 32]:
    37	        return MediaType.Video
    38	    if kind in [2, 4, 10, 14, 17, 21, 36]:
    39	        return MediaType.Music
    40	    if kind in [8, 64]:
    41	        return MediaType.TV
    42	
    43	    raise exceptions.UnknownMediaKindError("Unknown media kind: " + str(kind))
    44	
    45	
    46	def playstate(state: Optional[int]) -> DeviceState:
    47	    """Convert iTunes playstate to API representation."""
    48	    # pylint: disable=too-many-return-statements
    49	    if state == 0 or state is None:
    50	        return DeviceState.Idle
    51	    if state == 1:
    52	        return DeviceState.Loading
    53	    if state == 2:
    54	        return DeviceState.Stopped
    55	    if state == 3:
    56	        return DeviceState.Paused
    57	    if state == 4:
    58	        return DeviceState.Playing
    59	    if state in (5, 6):
    60	        return DeviceState.Seeking
    61	
    62	    raise exceptions.UnknownPlayStateError("Unknown playstate: " + str(state))
    63	
    64	
    65	def ms_to_s(time):
    66	    """Convert time in ms to seconds."""
    67	    if time is None:
    68	        return 0
    69	
    70	    # Happens in some special cases, just return 0
    71	    if time >= (2 ** 32 - 1):
    72	        return 0
    73	    return round(time / 1000.0)
    74	
    75	
    76	class DaapRequester:
    77	    """Helper class that makes it easy to perform DAAP requests.
    78	
    79	    It will automatically do login and other necessary book-keeping.
    80	    """
    81	
    82	    def __init__(self, http, login_id):
    83	        """Initialize a new DaapRequester."""
    84	        self.http = http
    85	        self._login_id = login_id
    86	        self._session_id = 0
    87	
    88	    async def login(self):
    89	        """Login to Apple TV using specified login id."""
    90	        # Do not use session.get_data(...) in login as that would end up in
    91	        # an infinite loop.
    92	        def _login_request():
    93	            url = self._mkurl("login?[AUTH]&hasFP=1", session=False, login_id=True)
    94	            _login_request.log_text = "Login request: " + url
    95	            return self.http.get_data(
    96	                url,
    97	                headers=_DMAP_HEADERS,
    98	            )
    99	
   100	        resp = await self._do(_login_request, is_login=True)
   101	        self._session_id = parser.first(resp, "mlog", "mlid")
   102	
   103	        _LOGGER.info("Logged in and got session id %s", self._session_id)
   104	        return self._session_id
   105	
   106	    async def get(self, cmd, daap_data=True, timeout=None, **args):
   107	        """Perform a DAAP GET command."""
   108	
   109	        def _get_request():
   110	            url = self._mkurl(cmd, *args)
   111	            _get_request.log_text = "GET request: " + url
   112	            return self.http.get_data(url, headers=_DMAP_HEADERS, timeout=timeout)
   113	
   114	        await self._assure_logged_in()
   115	        return await self._do(_get_request, is_daap=daap_data)
   116	
   117	    async def post(self, cmd, data=None, timeout=None, **args):
   118	        """Perform DAAP POST command with optional data."""
   119	
   120	        def _post_request():
   121	            url = self._mkurl(cmd, *args)
   122	            _post_request.log_text = "POST request: " + url
   123	            headers = copy(_DMAP_HEADERS)
   124	            headers["Content-Type"] = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded"
   125	            return self.http.post_data(url, data=data, headers=headers, timeout=timeout)
   126	
   127	        await self._assure_logged_in()
   128	        return await self._do(_post_request)
   129	
   130	    async def _do(self, action, retry=True, is_login=False, is_daap=True):
   131	        resp, status = await action()
   132	        if is_daap:
   133	            resp = parser.parse(resp, lookup_tag)
   134	
   135	        self._log_response(action.log_text, resp, is_daap)
   136	        if 200 <= status < 300:
   137	            return resp
   138	
   139	        # Seems to be the case?
   140	        if status == 500:
   141	            raise exceptions.NotSupportedError("command not supported at this stage")
   142	
   143	        if not is_login:
   144	            # If a request fails, try to login again before retrying
   145	            _LOGGER.info("implicitly logged out, logging in again")
   146	            await self.login()
   147	
   148	        # Retry once if we got a bad response, otherwise bail out
   149	        if retry:
   150	            return await self._do(action, False, is_login=is_login, is_daap=is_daap)
   151	
   152	        raise exceptions.AuthenticationError("failed to login: " + str(status))
   153	
   154	    def _mkurl(self, cmd, *args, session=True, login_id=False):
   155	        url = "{}".format(cmd.format(*args))
   156	        parameters = []
   157	        if login_id:
   158	            if re.match(r"0x[0-9A-Fa-f]{16}", self._login_id):
   159	                parameters.append("pairing-guid={}".format(self._login_id))
   160	            elif re.match(
   161	                r"[0-9A-Fa-f]{8}-([0-9A-Fa-f]{4}-){3}[0-9A-Fa-f]{12}", self._login_id
   162	            ):
   163	                parameters.append("hsgid={}".format(self._login_id))
   164	            else:
   165	                raise exceptions.InvalidCredentialsError(
   166	                    "invalid credentials: " + self._login_id
   167	                )
   168	        if session:
   169	            parameters.insert(0, "session-id={}".format(self._session_id))
   170	        return url.replace("[AUTH]", "&".join(parameters))
   171	
   172	    async def _assure_logged_in(self):
   173	        if self._session_id != 0:
   174	            _LOGGER.debug("Already logged in, re-using seasion id %d", self._session_id)
   175	        else:
   176	            await self.login()
   177	
   178	    @staticmethod
   179	    def _log_response(text, data, is_daap):
   180	        if _LOGGER.isEnabledFor(logging.INFO):
   181	            if is_daap:
   182	                _LOGGER.debug(parser.pprint(data, lookup_tag))
   183	            else:
   184	                log_binary(_LOGGER, text, Data=data)
   185

    93	
    94	def build_playing_instance(playstatus) -> Playing:
    95	    """Build a Playing instance from play state."""
    96	
    97	    def _get_time_in_seconds(tag) -> int:
    98	        time = parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", tag)
    99	        return daap.ms_to_s(time)
   100	
   101	    def media_type() -> MediaType:
   102	        """Type of media is currently playing, e.g. video, music."""
   103	        state = parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "caps")
   104	        if not state:
   105	            return MediaType.Unknown
   106	
   107	        mediakind = parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "cmmk")
   108	        if mediakind is not None:
   109	            return daap.media_kind(mediakind)
   110	
   111	        # Fallback: if artist or album exists we assume music (not present
   112	        # for video)
   113	        if artist() or album():
   114	            return MediaType.Music
   115	
   116	        return MediaType.Video
   117	
   118	    def device_state() -> DeviceState:
   119	        """Device state, e.g. playing or paused."""
   120	        state = parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "caps")
   121	        return daap.playstate(state)
   122	
   123	    def title() -> Optional[str]:
   124	        """Title of the current media, e.g. movie or song name."""
   125	        return parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "cann")
   126	
   127	    def artist() -> Optional[str]:
   128	        """Arist of the currently playing song."""
   129	        return parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "cana")
   130	
   131	    def album() -> Optional[str]:
   132	        """Album of the currently playing song."""
   133	        return parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "canl")
   134	
   135	    def genre() -> Optional[str]:
   136	        """Genre of the currently playing song."""
   137	        return parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "cang")
   138	
   139	    def total_time() -> Optional[int]:
   140	        """Total play time in seconds."""
   141	        return _get_time_in_seconds("cast")
   142	
   143	    def position() -> Optional[int]:
   144	        """Position in the playing media (seconds)."""
   145	        total = total_time()
   146	        remaining_time = _get_time_in_seconds("cant")
   147	        if not total or not remaining_time:
   148	            return None
   149	        return total - remaining_time
   150	
   151	    def shuffle() -> Optional[ShuffleState]:
   152	        """If shuffle is enabled or not."""
   153	        state = parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "cash")
   154	        if state is None or state == 0:
   155	            return ShuffleState.Off
   156	
   157	        # DMAP does not support the "albums" state and will always report
   158	        # "songs" if shuffle is active
   159	        return ShuffleState.Songs
   160	
   161	    def repeat() -> Optional[RepeatState]:
   162	        """Repeat mode."""
   163	        state = parser.first(playstatus, "cmst", "carp")
   164	        if state is None:
   165	            return RepeatState.Off
   166	        return RepeatState(state)
   167	
   168	    return Playing(
   169	        media_type=media_type(),
   170	        device_state=device_state(),
   171	        title=title(),
   172	        artist=artist(),
   173	        album=album(),
   174	        genre=genre(),
   175	        total_time=total_time(),
   176	        position=position(),
   177	        shuffle=shuffle(),
   178	        repeat=repeat(),
   179	    )

   182	class BaseDmapAppleTV:
   183	    """Common protocol logic used to interact with an Apple TV."""
   184	
   185	    def __init__(self, requester):
   186	        """Initialize a new Apple TV base implementation."""
   187	        self.daap = requester
   188	        self.playstatus_revision = 0
   189	        self.latest_playstatus = None
   190	        self.latest_playing = None
   191	        self.latest_hash = None
   192	
   193	    async def playstatus(self, use_revision=False, timeout=None):
   194	        """Request raw data about what is currently playing.
   195	
   196	        If use_revision=True, this command will "block" until playstatus
   197	        changes on the device.
   198	
   199	        Must be logged in.
   200	        """
   201	        cmd_url = _PSU_CMD.format(self.playstatus_revision if use_revision else 0)
   202	        resp = await self.daap.get(cmd_url, timeout=timeout)
   203	        self.playstatus_revision = parser.first(resp, "cmst", "cmsr")
   204	        self.latest_playstatus = resp
   205	        self.latest_playing = build_playing_instance(resp)
   206	        self.latest_hash = self.latest_playing.hash
   207	        return self.latest_playing
   208	
   209	    async def artwork(self, width, height) -> Optional[ArtworkInfo]:
   210	        """Return artwork for what is currently playing (or None)."""
   211	        url = _ARTWORK_CMD.format(width=width or 0, height=height or 0)
   212	        art = await self.daap.get(url, daap_data=False)
   213	        return art if art != b"" else None
   214	
   215	    def ctrl_int_cmd(self, cmd):
   216	        """Perform a "ctrl-int" command."""
   217	        cmd_url = "ctrl-int/1/{}?[AUTH]&prompt-id=0".format(cmd)
   218	        return self.daap.post(cmd_url)
   219	
   220	    def controlprompt_cmd(self, cmd):
   221	        """Perform a "controlpromptentry" command."""
   222	        data = tags.string_tag("cmbe", cmd) + tags.uint8_tag("cmcc", 0)
   223	        return self.daap.post(_CTRL_PROMPT_CMD, data=data)
   224	
   225	    def controlprompt_data(self, data):
   226	        """Perform a "controlpromptentry" command."""
   227	        return self.daap.post(_CTRL_PROMPT_CMD, data=data)
   228	
   229	    def set_property(self, prop, value):
   230	        """Change value of a DAAP property, e.g. volume or media position."""
   231	        cmd_url = "ctrl-int/1/setproperty?{}={}&[AUTH]".format(prop, value)
   232	        return self.daap.post(cmd_url)

   367	    async def skip_forward(self) -> None:
   368	        """Skip forward a time interval.
   369	
   370	        Skip interval is typically 15-30s, but is decided by the app.
   371	        """
   372	        current_position = (await self.apple_tv.playstatus()).position
   373	        if current_position:
   374	            await self.set_position(current_position + _DEFAULT_SKIP_TIME)
   375	
   376	    async def skip_backward(self) -> None:
   377	        """Skip backwards a time interval.
   378	
   379	        Skip interval is typically 15-30s, but is decided by the app.
   380	        """
   381	        current_position = (await self.apple_tv.playstatus()).position
   382	        if current_position:
   383	            await self.set_position(current_position - _DEFAULT_SKIP_TIME)
   384	
   385	    async def set_position(self, pos: int) -> None:
   386	        """Seek in the current playing media."""
   387	        time_in_ms = int(pos) * 1000
   388	        await self.apple_tv.set_property("dacp.playingtime", time_in_ms)

   390	    async def set_shuffle(self, shuffle_state: ShuffleState) -> None:
   391	        """Change shuffle mode to on or off."""
   392	        state = 0 if shuffle_state == ShuffleState.Off else 1
   393	        await self.apple_tv.set_property("dacp.shufflestate", state)
   394	
   395	    async def set_repeat(self, repeat_state: RepeatState) -> None:
   396	        """Change repeat mode."""
   397	        await self.apple_tv.set_property("dacp.repeatstate", repeat_state.value)

   400	class DmapMetadata(Metadata):
   401	    """Implementation of API for retrieving metadata from an Apple TV."""
   402	
   403	    def __init__(self, identifier, apple_tv):
   404	        """Initialize metadata instance."""
   405	        super().__init__(identifier)
   406	        self.apple_tv = apple_tv
   407	        self.artwork_cache = Cache(limit=4)
   408	
   409	    async def artwork(self, width=512, height=None) -> Optional[ArtworkInfo]:
   410	        """Return artwork for what is currently playing (or None).
   411	
   412	        The parameters "width" and "height" makes it possible to request artwork of a
   413	        specific size. This is just a request, the device might impose restrictions and
   414	        return artwork of a different size. Set both parameters to None to request
   415	        default size. Set one of them and let the other one be None to keep original
   416	        aspect ratio.
   417	        """
   418	        # Having to fetch "playing" here is not ideal, but an identifier is
   419	        # needed and we cannot trust any previous identifiers. So we have to do
   420	        # this until a better solution comes along.
   421	        playing = await self.playing()
   422	        identifier = playing.hash
   423	        if identifier in self.artwork_cache:
   424	            _LOGGER.debug("Retrieved artwork %s from cache", identifier)
   425	            return self.artwork_cache.get(identifier)
   426	
   427	        _LOGGER.debug("Fetching artwork")
   428	        artwork = await self.apple_tv.artwork(width, height)
   429	        if artwork:
   430	            info = ArtworkInfo(bytes=artwork, mimetype="image/png", width=-1, height=-1)
   431	            self.artwork_cache.put(identifier, info)
   432	            return info
   433	
   434	        return None
   435	
   436	    @property
   437	    def artwork_id(self):
   438	        """Return a unique identifier for current artwork."""
   439	        return self.apple_tv.latest_hash
   440	
   441	    async def playing(self):
   442	        """Return current device state."""
   443	        return await self.apple_tv.playstatus()
   444	
   445	    @property
   446	    def app(self) -> Optional[App]:
   447	        """Return information about running app."""
   448	        raise exceptions.NotSupportedError()

   461	        raise exceptions.NotSupportedError()

   468	class DmapPushUpdater(PushUpdater):
   469	    """Implementation of API for handling push update from an Apple TV."""
   470	
   471	    def __init__(self, loop, apple_tv, listener):
   472	        """Initialize a new DmapPushUpdater instance."""
   473	        super().__init__(loop)
   474	        self._atv = apple_tv
   475	        self._listener = weakref.ref(listener)
   476	        self._future = None
   477	        self._initial_delay = 0
   478	
   479	    @property
   480	    def active(self):
   481	        """Return if push updater has been started."""
   482	        return self._future is not None
   483	
   484	    def start(self, initial_delay=0):
   485	        """Wait for push updates from device.
   486	
   487	        Will throw NoAsyncListenerError if no listener has been set.
   488	        """
   489	        if self.listener is None:
   490	            raise exceptions.NoAsyncListenerError()
   491	        if self.active:
   492	            return
   493	
   494	        # Always start with 0 to trigger an immediate response for the
   495	        # first request
   496	        self._atv.playstatus_revision = 0
   497	
   498	        # Delay before restarting after an error
   499	        self._initial_delay = initial_delay
   500	
   501	        self._future = asyncio.ensure_future(self._poller(), loop=self.loop)
   502	
   503	    def stop(self):
   504	        """No longer forward updates to listener."""
   505	        if self._future is not None:
   506	            self._future.cancel()
   507	            self._future = None
   508	
   509	    async def _poller(self):
   510	        first_call = True
   511	
   512	        while True:
   513	            # Sleep some time before waiting for updates
   514	            if not first_call and self._initial_delay > 0:
   515	                _LOGGER.debug("Initial delay set to %d", self._initial_delay)
   516	                await asyncio.sleep(self._initial_delay, loop=self.loop)
   517	                first_call = False
   518	
   519	            try:
   520	                _LOGGER.debug("Waiting for playstatus updates")
   521	                playstatus = await self._atv.playstatus(use_revision=True, timeout=0)
   522	
   523	                self.post_update(playstatus)
   524	            except asyncio.CancelledError:
   525	                break
   526	
   527	            except ClientError as ex:
   528	                _LOGGER.exception("A communication error happened")
   529	                listener = self._listener()
   530	                if listener:
   531	                    self.loop.call_soon(listener.listener.connection_lost, ex)
   532	
   533	                break
   534	
   535	            # It is not pretty to disable pylint here, but we must catch _all_
   536	            # exceptions to keep the API.
   537	            except Exception as ex:  # pylint: disable=broad-except
   538	                _LOGGER.debug("Playstatus error occurred: %s", ex)
   539	                self.loop.call_soon(self.listener.playstatus_error, self, ex)
   540	
   541	        self._future = None
   542	
   543	
   544	class DmapFeatures(Features):
   545	    """Implementation of API for supported feature functionality."""

   548	        """Initialize a new DmapFeatures instance."""
   549	        self.config = config

   553	        self, feature_name: FeatureName
   554	    ) -> FeatureInfo:
   555	        """Return current state of a feature."""
   556	        if feature_name in _AVAILABLE_FEATURES:
   557	            return FeatureInfo(state=FeatureState.Available)
   558	        if feature_name in _UNKNOWN_FEATURES:
   559	            return FeatureInfo(state=FeatureState.Unknown)
   560	        if feature_name in _FIELD_FEATURES:

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me trace through the error flow more carefully. Looking at the logs, the issue is that when `_do` in `daap.py` gets a non-2xx status code, it:
1. Logs "implicitly logged out, logging in again"  
2. Calls `self.login()` to re-login
3. Retries the request once
4. If that fails too, throws AuthenticationError

But in the push updater, this exception is caught broadly and then the loop continues immediately without any delay, causing the rapid retry cycle.

Let me look at the exact flow:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the code flow:

1. In DmapPushUpdater._poller(), line 521 calls:
   `playstatus = await self._atv.playstatus(use_revision=True, timeout=0)`

2. This goes to BaseDmapAppleTV.playstatus() which calls:
   `resp = await self.daap.get(cmd_url, timeout=timeout)` 

3. This goes to DaapRequester.get() which calls:
   `await self._assure_logged_in()` and then `await self._do(_get_request, is_daap=daap_data)`

4. In DaapRequester._do(), when there's a non-success status code:
   - Line 145 logs "implicitly logged out, logging in again"
   - Line 146 calls `await self.login()` 
   - Line 149-150 retries once if retry=True
   - Line 152 raises AuthenticationError if retry fails

5. Back in DmapPushUpdater._poller(), the AuthenticationError is caught by the broad exception handler at line 537-539:
   ```python
   except Exception as ex:  # pylint: disable=broad-except
       _LOGGER.debug("Playstatus error occurred: %s", ex)
       self.loop.call_soon(self.listener.playstatus_error, self, ex)
   ```

6. The while loop at line 512 continues immediately with no delay, causing the rapid retry cycle.

The fix should introduce some kind of delay or backoff mechanism to prevent the tight loop. There are several approaches:
1. Add delay in the push updater when catching exceptions
2. Add delay in the DAAP layer when reconnection fails
3. Implement exponential backoff

Looking at the existing code, I see there's already an `_initial_delay` concept in DmapPushUpdater but it's only used conditionally and incorrectly (first_call logic seems flawed).

The cleanest approach would be to add some backoff mechanism in the push updater when errors occur, since that's where the polling loop lives.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me look more closely at the push updater logic and the retry mechanism:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/pyatv/dmap/__init__.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[509, 542]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/pyatv/dmap/__init__.py:

Let me create a reproduction script:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Script to reproduce the rapid retry loop issue described in the GitHub issue.

This script simulates the scenario where playstatus updates continuously fail,
causing the "implicitly logged out, logging in again" message to appear rapidly.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
from unittest.mock import MagicMock

from pyatv import connect
from pyatv.conf import DmapService, AppleTV
import ipaddress

from tests.fake_device import FakeAppleTV
from tests.fake_device.dmap import PlayingResponse

# Configure logging to see the rapid retry behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.DEBUG,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

# Reduce noise from other loggers
logging.getLogger('aiohttp').setLevel(logging.WARNING)
logging.getLogger('asyncio').setLevel(logging.WARNING)


class RapidRetryDetector:
    """Detects rapid retry patterns in logs."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.login_messages = []
        self.retry_start_time = None
        
    def detect_rapid_retry(self, message):
        """Detect if we're seeing rapid retry behavior."""
        if "Logged in and got session id" in message:
            current_time = asyncio.get_event_loop().time()
            self.login_messages.append(current_time)
            
            # Keep only recent messages (last 5 seconds)
            self.login_messages = [
                t for t in self.login_messages 
                if current_time - t <= 5.0
            ]
            
            # If we have many login messages in a short time, it's rapid retry
            if len(self.login_messages) >= 5:
                rate = len(self.login_messages) / 5.0  # logins per second
                if rate > 5:  # More than 5 logins per second is concerning
                    print(f"\n⚠️  RAPID RETRY DETECTED: {rate:.1f} logins/second")
                    return True
                    
        return False


class TestPushListener:
    """Listener to capture push updates and errors."""
    
    def __init__(self, detector):
        self.detector = detector
        self.update_count = 0
        self.error_count = 0
        
    def playstatus_update(self, updater, playstatus):
        """Called when playstatus updates successfully."""
        self.update_count += 1
        print(f"✅ Playstatus update #{self.update_count}")
        
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Called when playstatus encounters an error."""
        self.error_count += 1
        message = str(exception)
        print(f"❌ Playstatus error #{self.error_count}: {message}")
        
        # Detect rapid retry pattern
        if self.detector.detect_rapid_retry(message):
            print("🛑 Stopping test - rapid retry detected!")
            updater.stop()

"""
Script to reproduce the rapid retry loop issue described in the GitHub issue.

This script simulates the scenario where playstatus updates continuously fail,
causing the "implicitly logged out, logging in again" message to appear rapidly.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
from unittest.mock import MagicMock

from pyatv import connect
from pyatv.conf import DmapService, AppleTV
import ipaddress

from tests.fake_device import FakeAppleTV
from tests.fake_device.dmap import PlayingResponse

# Configure logging to see the rapid retry behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.DEBUG,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

# Reduce noise from other loggers
logging.getLogger('aiohttp').setLevel(logging.WARNING)
logging.getLogger('asyncio').setLevel(logging.WARNING)


class RapidRetryDetector:
    """Detects rapid retry patterns in logs."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.login_messages = []
        self.retry_start_time = None
        
    def detect_rapid_retry(self, message):
        """Detect if we're seeing rapid retry behavior."""
        if "Logged in and got session id" in message:
            current_time = asyncio.get_event_loop().time()
            self.login_messages.append(current_time)
            
            # Keep only recent messages (last 5 seconds)
            self.login_messages = [
                t for t in self.login_messages 
                if current_time - t <= 5.0
            ]
            
            # If we have many login messages in a short time, it's rapid retry
            if len(self.login_messages) >= 5:
                rate = len(self.login_messages) / 5.0  # logins per second
                if rate > 5:  # More than 5 logins per second is concerning
                    print(f"\n⚠️  RAPID RETRY DETECTED: {rate:.1f} logins/second")
                    return True
                    
        return False


class TestPushListener:
    """Listener to capture push updates and errors."""
    
    def __init__(self, detector):
        self.detector = detector
        self.update_count = 0
        self.error_count = 0
        
    def playstatus_update(self, updater, playstatus):
        """Called when playstatus updates successfully."""
        self.update_count += 1
        print(f"✅ Playstatus update #{self.update_count}")
        
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Called when playstatus encounters an error."""
        self.error_count += 1
        message = str(exception)
        print(f"❌ Playstatus error #{self.error_count}: {message}")
        
        # Detect rapid retry pattern
        if self.detector.detect_rapid_retry(message):
            print("🛑 Stopping test - rapid retry detected!")
            updater.stop()

"""
Script to reproduce the rapid retry loop issue described in the GitHub issue.

This script simulates the scenario where playstatus updates continuously fail,
causing the "implicitly logged out, logging in again" message to appear rapidly.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
import ipaddress

from pyatv import connect
from pyatv.conf import DmapService, AppleTV
from pyatv.const import Protocol

from tests.fake_device import FakeAppleTV
from tests.fake_device.dmap import PlayingResponse

# Configure logging to see the rapid retry behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

# Focus on our relevant loggers
logging.getLogger('pyatv.dmap.daap').setLevel(logging.DEBUG)
logging.getLogger('pyatv.dmap').setLevel(logging.DEBUG)

# Reduce noise from other loggers
logging.getLogger('aiohttp').setLevel(logging.WARNING)
logging.getLogger('asyncio').setLevel(logging.WARNING)


class TestPushListener:
    """Listener to capture push updates and errors."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.update_count = 0
        self.error_count = 0
        self.errors_seen = []
        
    def playstatus_update(self, updater, playstatus):
        """Called when playstatus updates successfully."""
        self.update_count += 1
        print(f"✅ Playstatus update #{self.update_count}")
        
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Called when playstatus encounters an error."""
        self.error_count += 1
        message = str(exception)
        self.errors_seen.append((asyncio.get_event_loop().time(), message))
        print(f"❌ Playstatus error #{self.error_count}: {message}")
        
        # Stop after too many errors to prevent infinite loop
        if self.error_count >= 20:
            print("🛑 Too many errors, stopping test!")
            updater.stop()


async def main():
    """Main test function to reproduce the issue."""
    print("🔧 Setting up fake Apple TV device...")
    
    # Constants matching the test setup
    HSGID = "12345678-6789-1111-2222-012345678911"
    PAIRING_GUID = "0x0000000000000001"
    SESSION_ID = 55555
    
    # Create fake device
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    fake_atv = FakeAppleTV(loop)
    state, usecase = fake_atv.add_service(
        Protocol.DMAP, 
        hsgid=HSGID, 
        pairing_guid=PAIRING_GUID, 
        session_id=SESSION_ID
    )
    
    # Start the fake device
    await fake_atv.start()
    
    try:
        print("📱 Connecting to fake Apple TV...")
        
        # Set up a scenario that will cause the issue:
        # 1. Successful login
        # 2. Playstatus updates that fail with 403 (simulating expired session)
        usecase.example_video(title="Test Video", paused=True)
        
        # Force the playstatus endpoint to return 403 errors
        # We do this by modifying the handler to simulate the exact error condition
        dmap_service_obj = fake_atv.services[Protocol.DMAP].service
        
        # Store original handler
        original_handler = None
        for resource in dmap_service_obj.app.router.resources():
            if str(resource.canonical) == '/ctrl-int/1/playstatusupdate':
                for route in resource._routes:
                    original_handler = route.handler
                    break
                break
        
        if original_handler:
            async def failing_playstatus_handler(request):
                """Handler that returns 403 to simulate session expiration."""
                # Check auth parameters first (this mimics real behavior)
                try:
                    # Verify auth like the original handler does
                    if 'session-id' not in request.query:
                        return web.Response(status=403)
                    
                    session_id = int(request.query['session-id'])
                    if session_id != SESSION_ID:
                        return web.Response(status=403)
                        
                    # Return 403 to trigger relogin logic
                    return web.Response(status=403, text="Forbidden")
                except Exception:
                    return web.Response(status=403)
            
            # Replace the route handler
            dmap_service_obj.app.router._resources.clear()
            dmap_service_obj.app.router.add_get("/login", dmap_service_obj.handle_login)
            dmap_service_obj.app.router.add_get("/ctrl-int/1/playstatusupdate", failing_playstatus_handler)
            dmap_service_obj.app.router.add_post("/ctrl-int/1/controlpromptentry", dmap_service_obj.handle_remote_button)
            dmap_service_obj.app.router.add_get("/ctrl-int/1/nowplayingartwork", dmap_service_obj.handle_artwork)
            dmap_service_obj.app.router.add_post("/ctrl-int/1/setproperty", dmap_service_obj.handle_set_property)
        
        # Set up device configuration
        dmap_service = DmapService("dmapid", HSGID, port=fake_atv.port)
        conf = AppleTV(ipaddress.IPv4Address("127.0.0.1"), "Test Apple TV")
        conf.add_service(dmap_service)
        
        # Connect to the device
        atv = await connect(conf, loop)
        await atv.connect()  # Explicit connect to trigger login
        
        print("🚀 Starting push updater to trigger the issue...")
        
        # Set up listener
        listener = TestPushListener()
        atv.push_updater.listener = listener
        
        # Start push updates
        atv.push_updater.start()
        
        # Let it run for a bit to observe the behavior
        print("⏳ Observing behavior for 5 seconds...")
        await asyncio.sleep(5)
        
        # Stop everything
        atv.push_updater.stop()
        atv.close()
        
        print(f"\n📊 Results:")
        print(f"   Updates received: {listener.update_count}")
        print(f"   Errors encountered: {listener.error_count}")
        
        if listener.error_count > 5:  # Should see rapid errors
            print("🚨 Issue reproduced: High error rate indicates rapid retry behavior")
            
            # Show timing of errors to demonstrate rapid retry
            if len(listener.errors_seen) >= 2:
                times = [t for t, _ in listener.errors_seen[-10:]]  # Last 10 errors
                if len(times) >= 2:
                    intervals = [times[i+1] - times[i] for i in range(len(times)-1)]
                    if intervals:
                        avg_interval = sum(intervals) / len(intervals)
                        rate = 1.0 / avg_interval if avg_interval > 0 else float('inf')
                        print(f"   Average error interval: {avg_interval:.3f}s ({rate:.1f} errors/second)")
                        if rate > 2:  # More than 2 per second is rapid
                            print("   🔥 CONFIRMED: Rapid retry loop detected!")
        else:
            print("✅ No rapid retry detected")
            
    finally:
        await fake_atv.stop()

    from aiohttp import web

PAIRING_GUID = "0x0000000000000001"
SESSION_ID = 55555


async def main():
    """Reproduce the rapid retry issue."""
    print("Setting up fake Apple TV...")
    
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    fake_atv = FakeAppleTV(loop)
    state, usecase = fake_atv.add_service(
        Protocol.DMAP,
        hsgid=HSGID,
        pairing_guid=PAIRING_GUID,
        session_id=SESSION_ID
    )
    
    # Configure to return 403 for playstatus updates to trigger the issue
    state.playing = PlayingResponse(artwork_status=403)  # This might work
    
    await fake_atv.start()
    
    try:
        print("Connecting to device...")
        
        # Set up some content first
        usecase.example_video(title="Test", paused=True)
        
        # Force artwork to fail with 403 (similar to playstatus)
        usecase.artwork_no_permission()  # This sets artwork_status = 403
        
        dmap_service = DmapService("dmap_id", HSGID, port=fake_atv.port)
        conf = AppleTV(ipaddress.IPv4Address("127.0.0.1"), "Test Apple TV")
        conf.add_service(dmap_service)
        
        atv = await connect(conf, loop)
        await atv.connect()
        
        print("Connected! Trying to trigger the relogin loop...")
        
        # Try to access something that will fail and trigger relogin
        try:
            artwork = await atv.metadata.artwork()
            print(f"Artwork result: {artwork}")
        except Exception as ex:
            print(f"Exception (expected): {ex}")
            
        # Now let's manually create the scenario that causes the push updater issue
        # We need to make playstatus updates fail with 403
        
        # Let's override the playstatus handler directly
        dmap_svc = fake_atv.services[Protocol.DMAP].service
        
        # Find and replace the playstatus handler
        routes_to_remove = []
        for resource in list(dmap_svc.app.router._resources):
            if hasattr(resource, '_route') and resource._route:
                if 'playstatusupdate' in str(resource._route.path):
                    routes_to_remove.append(resource)
            elif hasattr(resource, '_routes'):
                for route in resource._routes:
                    if 'playstatusupdate' in str(route.path):
                        routes_to_remove.append(resource)
                        break
                        
        # Clear routes and re-add with our custom handler
        router = dmap_svc.app.router
        router._resources.clear()
        router._named_resources.clear()
        
        async def custom_playstatus_handler(request):
            print("Custom handler called - returning 403")
            return web.Response(status=403)
            
        router.add_get("/login", dmap_svc.handle_login)
        router.add_get("/ctrl-int/1/playstatusupdate", custom_playstatus_handler)
        router.add_post("/ctrl-int/1/controlpromptentry", dmap_svc.handle_remote_button)
        router.add_get("/ctrl-int/1/nowplayingartwork", dmap_svc.handle_artwork)
        router.add_post("/ctrl-int/1/setproperty", dmap_svc.handle_set_property)
        
        # Test with push updater
        class Listener:
            def playstatus_update(self, updater, playstatus):
                print("Got playstatus update")
                
            def playstatus_error(self, updater, ex):
                print(f"Playstatus error: {ex}")
        
        listener = Listener()
        atv.push_updater.listener = listener
        atv.push_updater.start()
        
        print("Started push updater - watching for rapid retries...")
        await asyncio.sleep(3)
        
        atv.push_updater.stop()
        atv.close()
        
    finally:
        await fake_atv.stop()

Let me create a much simpler approach by directly testing the push updater behavior:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/test_rapid_retry.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Direct test of the rapid retry issue in push updater.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, Mock

from pyatv.dmap import DmapPushUpdater
from pyatv.exceptions import AuthenticationError

# Enable logging to see the behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.DEBUG,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class MockAppleTV:
    """Mock Apple TV that simulates failing playstatus calls."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.playstatus_revision = 0
        self.call_count = 0
        
    async def playstatus(self, use_revision=True, timeout=None):
        """Simulate playstatus that always fails."""
        self.call_count += 1
        logger.info(f"Playstatus call #{self.call_count}")
        
        # Always raise AuthenticationError to simulate the 403/406 issue
        raise AuthenticationError("failed to login: 403")


class MockListener:
    """Mock listener to capture events."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.error_count = 0
        self.errors = []
        
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Handle playstatus errors."""
        self.error_count += 1
        self.errors.append(asyncio.get_event_loop().time())
        logger.error(f"Playstatus error #{self.error_count}: {exception}")
        
        # Stop after too many errors
        if self.error_count >= 15:
            logger.error("Too many errors, stopping!")
            updater.stop()


async def test_rapid_retry():
    """Test the rapid retry behavior."""
    print("🧪 Testing rapid retry behavior in push updater...")
    
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    
    # Create mock components
    mock_atv = MockAppleTV()
    mock_listener = MockListener()
    
    # Create push updater
    updater = DmapPushUpdater(loop, mock_atv, mock_listener)
    
    # Start the updater
    print("🚀 Starting push updater...")
    updater.start()
    
    # Let it run for a few seconds to observe behavior
    print("⏳ Running for 3 seconds to observe retry pattern...")
    await asyncio.sleep(3)
    
    # Stop the updater
    updater.stop()
    
    # Analyze results
    print(f"\n📊 Results:")
    print(f"   Total playstatus calls: {mock_atv.call_count}")
    print(f"   Total errors reported: {mock_listener.error_count}")
    
    # Calculate call rate
    if len(mock_listener.errors) >= 2:
        # Calculate average interval between errors
        intervals = [
            mock_listener.errors[i+1] - mock_listener.errors[i] 
            for i in range(len(mock_listener.errors)-1)
        ]
        if intervals:
            avg_interval = sum(intervals) / len(intervals)
            calls_per_second = 1.0 / avg_interval if avg_interval > 0 else float('inf')
            print(f"   Average interval: {avg_interval:.4f}s")
            print(f"   Rate: {calls_per_second:.1f} calls/second")
            
            if calls_per_second > 5:
                print("   🔥 RAPID RETRY DETECTED!")
                print("   🐛 This confirms the bug described in the issue.")
                return True
            else:
                print("   ✅ Normal retry rate")
                return False
    
    print("   ❓ Insufficient data")
    return False


if __name__ == "__main__":
    result = asyncio.run(test_rapid_retry())
    if result:
        print("\n🎯 Issue successfully reproduced!")
    else:
        print("\n✅ No issue detected")</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/test_rapid_retry.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7 && python test_rapid_retry.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>2025-09-02 08:58:50,009 DEBUG [pyatv.dmap] Waiting for playstatus updates

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a clear understanding of the issue:

**Problem Location:** 
In `/workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/pyatv/dmap/__init__.py`, in the `DmapPushUpdater._poller()` method (lines 509-541).

**What the Problem Is:**
The push updater enters an infinite tight loop when playstatus requests fail:
1. `_poller()` calls `self._atv.playstatus(use_revision=True, timeout=0)` 
2. This eventually leads to `DaapRequester._do()` getting a non-2xx status code
3. `_do()` logs "implicitly logged out, logging in again" and calls `self.login()`
4. Then it retries the request once
5. If that also fails, it raises `AuthenticationError`
6. Back in `_poller()`, this exception is caught by the broad `except Exception` handler
7. The handler logs the error and calls `self.listener.playstatus_error()`
8. The `while True` loop immediately continues without ANY delay
9. This creates an infinite rapid retry loop with no throttling

**How My Test Reproduces the Issue:**
My test creates a mock AppleTV that always throws `AuthenticationError` when `playstatus()` is called. This simulates the 403/406 errors seen in the real logs. The push updater then goes into the tight retry loop.

**Best Practices for the Fix:**
1. Introduce exponential backoff or at least a minimum delay when errors occur
2. Don't break legitimate quick reconnect capability for transient network issues  
3. Follow existing patterns in the codebase
4. Make the fix minimal and focused
5. Consider that some errors might be temporary and deserve quick retries, but persistent errors need throttling

**How to Fix the Problem:**
Add a delay mechanism in the `DmapPushUpdater._poller()` method when exceptions are caught. The cleanest approach would be to:

1. Add a delay counter that increases with consecutive errors
2. Apply exponential backoff (e.g., start with 1 second, then 2, 4, 8, up to a maximum)
3. Reset the delay counter on successful requests
4. Apply the delay before continuing the loop after error handling

Looking at the existing code, I see there's already an `_initial_delay` parameter but it's implemented incorrectly (the logic sets `first_call = False` AFTER the sleep check, and only applies it conditionally).

A better approach would be to:
1. Track consecutive error count
2. Apply increasing delays based on error count
3. Reset error count on successful requests</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The `DmapPushUpdater._poller()` method enters a tight infinite retry loop when playstatus requests fail, causing 1000+ requests per second with no delay between retries.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
File: `/workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/pyatv/dmap/__init__.py`
Method: `DmapPushUpdater._poller()` (lines 509-541)
Specifically the `while True` loop that catches exceptions but continues immediately without delay.

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
My test creates a mock AppleTV that always throws `AuthenticationError` when `playstatus()` is called, simulating the 403/406 HTTP errors from real Apple TVs. This triggers the rapid retry behavior where the push updater continuously retries without any throttling.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Implement exponential backoff to gradually increase delay between retries
- Reset backoff counter on successful requests  
- Preserve legitimate quick reconnect capability for transient issues
- Make minimal, focused changes
- Follow existing code patterns and conventions

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem
Add exponential backoff logic in `DmapPushUpdater._poller()`:
1. Track consecutive error count
2. Apply increasing delays (1s, 2s, 4s, 8s, max 30s) based on error count
3. Reset error count on successful requests
4. Insert `await asyncio.sleep(delay)` before continuing the loop after errors

        first_call = True

        while True:
            # Sleep some time before waiting for updates
            if not first_call and self._initial_delay > 0:
                _LOGGER.debug("Initial delay set to %d", self._initial_delay)
                await asyncio.sleep(self._initial_delay, loop=self.loop)
                first_call = False

            try:
                _LOGGER.debug("Waiting for playstatus updates")
                playstatus = await self._atv.playstatus(use_revision=True, timeout=0)

                self.post_update(playstatus)
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                break

            except ClientError as ex:
                _LOGGER.exception("A communication error happened")
                listener = self._listener()
                if listener:
                    self.loop.call_soon(listener.listener.connection_lost, ex)

                break

            # It is not pretty to disable pylint here, but we must catch _all_
            # exceptions to keep the API.
            except Exception as ex:  # pylint: disable=broad-except
                _LOGGER.debug("Playstatus error occurred: %s", ex)
                self.loop.call_soon(self.listener.playstatus_error, self, ex)

        self._future = None</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    async def _poller(self):
        first_call = True

        while True:
            # Sleep some time before waiting for updates
            if not first_call and self._initial_delay > 0:
                _LOGGER.debug("Initial delay set to %d", self._initial_delay)
                await asyncio.sleep(self._initial_delay, loop=self.loop)
            first_call = False

            try:
                _LOGGER.debug("Waiting for playstatus updates")
                playstatus = await self._atv.playstatus(use_revision=True, timeout=0)

                # Reset consecutive error count on success
                self._consecutive_errors = 0
                
                self.post_update(playstatus)
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                break

            except ClientError as ex:
                _LOGGER.exception("A communication error happened")
                listener = self._listener()
                if listener:
                    self.loop.call_soon(listener.listener.connection_lost, ex)

                break

            # It is not pretty to disable pylint here, but we must catch _all_
            # exceptions to keep the API.
            except Exception as ex:  # pylint: disable=broad-except
                _LOGGER.debug("Playstatus error occurred: %s", ex)
                self.loop.call_soon(self.listener.playstatus_error, self, ex)
                
                # Apply exponential backoff for consecutive errors
                self._consecutive_errors += 1
                if self._consecutive_errors > 1:
                    # Exponential backoff: 1s, 2s, 4s, 8s, 16s, 30s (max)
                    delay = min(2 ** (self._consecutive_errors - 2), 30)
                    _LOGGER.debug("Consecutive errors: %d, waiting %ds before retry", 
                                self._consecutive_errors, delay)
                    await asyncio.sleep(delay, loop=self.loop)

        self._future = None</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/postlund__pyatv__0.7/pyatv/dmap/__init__.py has been edited. """
Test script to verify the fix works correctly.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, Mock

from pyatv.dmap import DmapPushUpdater
from pyatv.exceptions import AuthenticationError

# Enable logging to see the behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.DEBUG,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class MockAppleTV:
    """Mock Apple TV that simulates failing playstatus calls."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.playstatus_revision = 0
        self.call_count = 0
        
    async def playstatus(self, use_revision=True, timeout=None):
        """Simulate playstatus that always fails."""
        self.call_count += 1
        logger.info(f"Playstatus call #{self.call_count}")
        
        # Always raise AuthenticationError to simulate the 403/406 issue
        raise AuthenticationError("failed to login: 403")


class MockListener:
    """Mock listener to capture events."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.error_count = 0
        self.errors = []
        
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Handle playstatus errors."""
        self.error_count += 1
        self.errors.append(asyncio.get_event_loop().time())
        logger.error(f"Playstatus error #{self.error_count}: {exception}")


async def test_fixed_behavior():
    """Test that the fix prevents rapid retry."""
    print("🧪 Testing fixed behavior in push updater...")
    
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    
    # Create mock components
    mock_atv = MockAppleTV()
    mock_listener = MockListener()
    
    # Create push updater
    updater = DmapPushUpdater(loop, mock_atv, mock_listener)
    
    # Start the updater
    print("🚀 Starting push updater...")
    updater.start()
    
    # Let it run for 10 seconds to observe behavior
    print("⏳ Running for 10 seconds to observe retry pattern...")
    await asyncio.sleep(10)
    
    # Stop the updater
    updater.stop()
    
    # Analyze results
    print(f"\n📊 Results:")
    print(f"   Total playstatus calls: {mock_atv.call_count}")
    print(f"   Total errors reported: {mock_listener.error_count}")
    
    # Calculate call rate
    if len(mock_listener.errors) >= 2:
        # Look at the last few errors to see the pattern
        recent_errors = mock_listener.errors[-10:] if len(mock_listener.errors) >= 10 else mock_listener.errors
        if len(recent_errors) >= 2:
            intervals = [
                recent_errors[i+1] - recent_errors[i] 
                for i in range(len(recent_errors)-1)
            ]
            if intervals:
                avg_interval = sum(intervals) / len(intervals)
                calls_per_second = 1.0 / avg_interval if avg_interval > 0 else float('inf')
                print(f"   Recent average interval: {avg_interval:.2f}s")
                print(f"   Recent rate: {calls_per_second:.2f} calls/second")
                
                if calls_per_second < 2:
                    print("   ✅ GOOD: Proper backoff applied!")
                    print("   🎉 Fix is working correctly.")
                    return True
                else:
                    print("   ❌ BAD: Still rapid retrying!")
                    return False
    
    print("   ❓ Insufficient data")
    return False

                return False

1. **Call #1**: No delay (first attempt)
2. **Call #2**: 1 second delay (2^(2-2) = 1s)
3. **Call #3**: 2 second delay (2^(3-2) = 2s) 
4. **Call #4**: 4 second delay (2^(4-2) = 4s)
5. **Call #5**: 8 second delay (2^(5-2) = 8s)
6. **Call #6**: 16 second delay (2^(6-2) = 16s)

This perfectly matches the exponential backoff formula I implemented: `delay = min(2 ** (errors - 2), 30)` with a maximum of 30 seconds.

"""
Test that the backoff resets after successful calls.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, Mock

from pyatv.dmap import DmapPushUpdater
from pyatv.exceptions import AuthenticationError
from pyatv.interface import Playing

# Enable logging to see the behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.DEBUG,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class MockAppleTV:
    """Mock Apple TV that simulates mixed success/failure."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.playstatus_revision = 0
        self.call_count = 0
        self.fail_count = 0
        self.success_count = 0
        
    async def playstatus(self, use_revision=True, timeout=None):
        """Simulate playstatus with controlled success/failure."""
        self.call_count += 1
        logger.info(f"Playstatus call #{self.call_count}")
        
        # Fail first 3 times, then succeed once, then fail again
        if self.call_count <= 3 or (self.call_count >= 5 and self.call_count <= 7):
            self.fail_count += 1
            logger.info(f"Failing call #{self.call_count}")
            raise AuthenticationError("failed to login: 403")
        else:
            self.success_count += 1
            logger.info(f"Succeeding call #{self.call_count}")
            # Return a mock playing object
            mock_playing = Mock()
            mock_playing.hash = "test_hash"
            return mock_playing


class MockListener:
    """Mock listener to capture events."""
    
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Handle playstatus errors."""
        logger.error(f"Playstatus error: {exception}")
        
    def playstatus_update(self, updater, playstatus):
        """Handle playstatus updates."""
        logger.info("Playstatus update received")


async def test_backoff_reset():
    """Test that backoff resets after successful calls."""
    print("🧪 Testing backoff reset behavior...")
    
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    
    # Create mock components
    mock_atv = MockAppleTV()
    mock_listener = MockListener()
    
    # Create push updater
    updater = DmapPushUpdater(loop, mock_atv, mock_listener)
    
    # Start the updater
    print("🚀 Starting push updater...")
    updater.start()
    
    # Let it run for enough time to see the pattern
    print("⏳ Running to observe backoff and reset...")
    await asyncio.sleep(25)
    
    # Stop the updater
    updater.stop()
    
    # Analyze results
    print(f"\n📊 Results:")
    print(f"   Total calls: {mock_atv.call_count}")
    print(f"   Failed calls: {mock_atv.fail_count}")
    print(f"   Success calls: {mock_atv.success_count}")
    
    print("✅ Test completed")


if __name__ == "__main__":

"""
Test that the backoff resets after successful calls - simplified version.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
from unittest.mock import Mock

from pyatv.dmap import DmapPushUpdater
from pyatv.exceptions import AuthenticationError
from pyatv.interface import Playing

# Enable logging to see the behavior
logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format='%(asctime)s %(levelname)s [%(name)s] %(message)s'
)

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class MockAppleTV:
    """Mock Apple TV that simulates controlled success/failure pattern."""
    
    def __init__(self):
        self.playstatus_revision = 0
        self.call_count = 0
        self.fail_streak = 0
        self.max_fails = 4  # Fail this many times, then succeed once
        
    async def playstatus(self, use_revision=True, timeout=None):
        """Simulate playstatus with controlled pattern."""
        self.call_count += 1
        
        # Every N calls, we fail for a streak, then succeed
        cycle_position = (self.call_count - 1) % (self.max_fails + 1)
        
        if cycle_position < self.max_fails:
            # Fail for the first N calls in each cycle
            self.fail_streak += 1
            logger.info(f"Playstatus call #{self.call_count} - FAIL streak #{self.fail_streak}")
            raise AuthenticationError("failed to login: 403")
        else:
            # Succeed on the last call in each cycle
            logger.info(f"Playstatus call #{self.call_count} - SUCCESS (resets backoff)")
            # Return a mock playing object
            mock_playing = Mock()
            mock_playing.hash = "test_hash"
            self.fail_streak = 0  # Reset fail streak on success
            return mock_playing


class MockListener:
    """Mock listener to capture events."""
    
    def playstatus_error(self, updater, exception):
        """Handle playstatus errors."""
        logger.info(f"Playstatus error callback")
        
    def playstatus_update(self, updater, playstatus):
        """Handle playstatus updates."""
        logger.info("Playstatus update callback")


async def test_backoff_behavior():
    """Test the backoff behavior and reset."""
    print("🧪 Testing backoff behavior with reset...")
    
    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    
    # Create mock components
    mock_atv = MockAppleTV()
    mock_listener = MockListener()
    
    # Create push updater
    updater = DmapPushUpdater(loop, mock_atv, mock_listener)
    
    # Start the updater
    print("🚀 Starting push updater...")
    updater.start()
    
    # Let it run for enough time to see multiple cycles
    print("⏳ Running to observe backoff pattern and reset...")
    await asyncio.sleep(45)  # Should see several complete cycles
    
    # Stop the updater
    updater.stop()
    
    # Analyze results
    print(f"\n📊 Results:")
    print(f"   Total calls: {mock_atv.call_count}")
    print(f"   Current fail streak: {mock_atv.fail_streak}")
    
    print("✅ Test completed - check logs for backoff pattern")
